# HEXA-0POD

@title: 🔁 HEXA-0POD — flame+forge improvement loop on FREE resources only (no vast pod)

@goal: Continuously improve flame + forge using ONLY free resources — the sidecar pool (aiden RTX 5070
sm_120, summer RTX 5070, pi5-akida, ghost) + local CPU/code work. ZERO vast rentals. Each round: pick a
0-pod-feasible improvement, do it, verify on a free pool GPU (byte-eq / bit-exact gates), land it, loop.
Hopper-sm_90a-only work (the wgmma decode-elim own-GEMM) is OUT-OF-SCOPE here (needs an H100 pod); this
loop targets what the consumer card + code can carry.

## milestones (loop self-feeds; add as discovered)

- [x] **OP-139 — byte-producing escape/codec-decoder multibyte sweep (OP-128/135/136 cross-token class, applied to the ESCAPE decoder surface): 🔴 1 BYTE-decoder FIXED + 🟢 CONVERGED (18 decoders surveyed / 5 byte-producing string-builders / 1 buggy / 1 fixed).** HUNT (LANE-2): swept every escape/codec decoder that "parses an escape → produces a raw byte 0-255 → appends" (`%XX`, `\xHH`, base64, octal, `=XX` QP) vs every codepoint decoder (`\uXXXX`, `&#…`). RULE: a BYTE escape decodes to a RAW byte → must accumulate bytes then `bytes_to_str_raw` ONCE; `from_char_code(byte)` per byte re-UTF-8-encodes every byte ≥0x80 as code point U+00xx = MOJIBAKE (runtime-DIVERGENT: interpreter `chr(b)` is byte-faithful but `from_char_code(b)` is codepoint). 🔴 BUG — `self/runtime/base64_pure.hexa::base64_decode_pure` built its result string via `out_chars.push(from_char_code(byte))` per decoded byte → every multibyte base64 payload mojibaked: `base64_decode_pure("7ZWc6riA8J+YgA==")` (base64 of `한글😀`, 10 raw bytes) gave 20-byte garbage instead of `한글😀` (verified vs Python `base64.b64decode`). FIX = accumulate into `out_bytes` then `bytes_to_str_raw` once — byte-identical to `url_pure.url_decode_pure` (OP-136) and `stdlib/codec/base64.base64_decode_str`. 🟢 EVERY other byte decoder is already CORRECT: the hxc `_dict/_enum/_body_unescape` `\xHH` decoders use `chr()` (byte-faithful, +the paired encoder only emits `\x00-\x7f`); `encoding_pure.base64_decode_pure`, `stdlib/codec/base64.base64_decode`, and all `*hex_to_bytes` return raw `[int]` arrays (no per-byte stringify); `encoding_pure.hex_dump_pure`'s `from_char_code` is guarded to 0x20-0x7E; `runtime_pure.rt_str_to_upper/lower` are ASCII case-shift (<0x80). Codepoint decoders (`\uXXXX`: lsp · qwen_bpe · json_mini · http_server · rt/json) use `from_char_code(codepoint)` CORRECTLY — astral surrogate-pair combination is a DOCUMENTED BMP-only scope (lsp/qwen comment it), not the byte-class bug; `rt/json` passes `\u` through 6 bytes verbatim; `utf8_from_codepoints` is surrogate-validated (utf8_test 🟢). GUARD: hermetic `self/runtime/base64_pure_multibyte_op139_test.hexa` `__HEXA_BASE64_PURE_MULTIBYTE_OP139__ 6/6` / `PASS` — fixed body byte-exact vs Python oracle (한글😀 · café✓ · Man · empty) + a negative control asserting the OLD per-byte `from_char_code` body mojibakes the same input (10→20 bytes); the test INLINES both bodies because `use "self/runtime/base64_pure"` resolves to the INSTALLED Jun-1 stdlib copy (the shipped fix is byte-identical to the inlined `base64_decode_fixed`). CLOSURE/FLAGS: `base64_pure.hexa` is a DORMANT runtime-port leaf — ZERO importer in the compiler build closure (grep compiler/ = 0; only its own tests `use` it) → closure-OUT, ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact; net-additive (1 body rewrite + new test, 0 deletions) → wipe_guard N/A. STALE-BINARY CAVEAT: local Jun-1 hexa's `use` resolves the installed stdlib, not the worktree edit — fix verified via inlined-oracle proof (the exact pattern OP-136 used for the dormant url_pure/encoding_pure fixes); rebuild-pending only for a full-closure import run. ONE HONEST LINE: OP-136 fixed the 3 percent-decoders, OP-139 fixes the one remaining byte-decoder (base64_decode_pure) which mojibaked every multibyte base64 payload — it now round-trips byte-exact vs Python base64; every other byte decoder is chr()-byte-faithful or returns a raw `[int]` array, and every `\u` decoder is codepoint-correct. LANE-2. $0 · 0-pod · NO GPU · no vast · no foreign-pod · leak-0. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP139-BYTE-ESCAPE-DECODER-SWEEP.txt.
- [x] **OP-136 — URL/URI parse + percent-decode + authority decomposition value-correctness & SECURITY audit: 🔴 2 bugs FIXED (1 VALUE/encoding · 1 SECURITY) + 🟢 CONVERGED (5 URL fns audited / 4 buggy / 4 fixed; path_normalize CLEAN, already OP-116-locked).** HUNT (LANE-2): there is NO general scheme://authority URL parser in-repo (no url_parse/uri_parse/resolve_uri/url_join) — the surface is the OP-91 query helper (parse_query/parse_request, no %-decode/normalize, out of scope), the percent codecs (`self/runtime/url_pure.hexa` + `encoding_pure.hexa` `url_decode_pure`, `self/main.hexa` `hexa_url_decode`, `self/std_encoding.hexa` `url_decode`), the ws `_ws_url_parse` (the ONLY scheme://authority decomposer), and the filesystem `stdlib/alloc/path.hexa::path_normalize`. Each tested vs a Python urllib.parse oracle. 🔴 BUG 1 (VALUE/encoding · OP-128 cross-token class) — a %XX escape encodes ONE RAW BYTE, but 3 decoders emitted `from_char_code(byte)`/`chr(byte)` PER ESCAPE, so under the Rust interpreter every byte ≥0x80 was re-UTF-8-encoded as code point U+00xx: `url_decode("%E2%9C%93")` (✓) gave the 6-byte garbage `c3 a2 c2 9c c2 93` instead of the 3 raw bytes `e2 9c 93` (verified vs `unquote`). FIX = collect raw bytes → `bytes_to_str_raw` (the OP-135 byte-faithful SSOT; the exact pattern `std_encoding.url_decode` already used) → correct under BOTH interpreter and self-host codegen (where chr→hexa_chr_byte &0xFF was coincidentally byte-faithful — a runtime-DIVERGENT bug). 🔴 BUG 2 (SECURITY — parse confusion / DoS) — `_ws_url_parse` split host:port on the FIRST ':'. An IPv6 literal `[::1]:8080` is full of ':' → host parsed to `"["` and the port read `to_int(":1]:8080")` which THREW `to_int: not an integer` → EVERY IPv6 `ws://` URL HARD-CRASHED the parser (parse-confusion DoS; under a permissive to_int it would have silently mis-routed host="["/wrong-port). FIX = bracket-aware split (RFC 3986 §3.2.2): host = the bracketed span (brackets stripped, matching `urlsplit().hostname`), port = the ':' AFTER ']' only → `ws://[::1]:8080/p` now host "::1" port 8080, byte-matching urlsplit on all 6 cases. 🟢 `path_normalize` RE-VERIFIED CLEAN vs posixpath: `/../`→`/`, `/../../etc/passwd`→`/etc/passwd`, `/a/../../b`→`/b` — a `..` can NEVER escape an absolute root (no traversal; already OP-116-locked; filesystem semantics, posixpath is the correct oracle). The leaf %-decoder of `%2F`→`/` is safe (the ws parser splits the path BEFORE this decoder runs → no premature-reserved-decode confusion). The `i+2 < n` bounds guard AUDITED correct (trailing complete escape decodes, partial verbatim). GUARD: hermetic `stdlib/net/url_parse_normalize_op136_test.hexa` 16/16 PASS (`__HEXA_STDLIB_URL_PARSE_NORMALIZE_OP136__ 16/16` / PASS), both fixes locked vs oracle + EACH with a negative-control falsifier (OLD per-escape body corrupts ✓; OLD first-':' split → host "[" inside `[::1]:8080`); `test_url_pure.hexa` 23/23 PASS with the fixed body. CLOSURE/FLAGS: ALL edited files are closure-OUT — `url_pure`/`encoding_pure` DORMANT (self-test-only), `self/main.hexa` = the OLD self-host tree (active build compiles `compiler/main.hexa`, 0 `use "self/main"`), `websocket_native.hexa` = net leaf (parses clean through `websocket.hexa` closure) → ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact; net-additive (4 corrected bodies + new test, 0 deletions) → wipe_guard N/A; NO .tape. STALE-BINARY CAVEAT: local Jun-7 hexa rejects pre-existing `nil` in encoding_pure's UNRELATED base64 decoder (NOT my hunks — they add no `nil`); that file's fix is structurally identical to the oracle-verified url_pure fix (rebuild-pending only for its full-closure run). ONE HONEST LINE: no general URL parser exists; of the codec+ws surface that does, multibyte %-decode silently corrupted all non-ASCII UTF-8 (3 decoders) and the ws parser hard-crashed on every IPv6 URL — both now byte-match urllib.parse; the lone path normalizer was already traversal-safe. LANE-2. $0 · 0-pod · NO GPU · no vast · no foreign-pod · leak-0. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP136-URL-PARSE-NORMALIZE.txt.

REPRODUCED on the binary (seed s=0x0000000100000001): BEFORE step1 (hi,lo)=(475707226, 4249649341) ≠ reference; AFTER fix=(2229881178, 4249649341)= canonical, and the full 5-draw sequence is byte-EXACT vs the numpy-uint64 oracle. The comment's false arithmetic (`0x2545F491 = 625058449`) was also corrected. 🟢 EVERYTHING ELSE CLEAN: (1) `stdlib/core/math/rng.hexa::lcg_next` (Numerical Recipes LCG a=1664525 c=1013904223 m=2^32) reproduces seed=42→1083814273,378494188,2479403867… byte-exact vs oracle — already reference-locked by the existing `stdlib/math/rng_test.hexa` (re-verified PASS 11/11); (2) the flame TRAINING-INIT LCGs `flame_math.dt_lcg_next` + `tensor_lib._flame_lcg_next` (MMIX 64-bit mult 6364136223846793005) reproduce byte-exact AND are platform-independent (two's-comp wrap + C-mod verified: 1151595062,1116573791…) — THIS is the bit-exact training path and it is sound; (3) `weave_composition.lcg_shuffle` is the CORRECT unbiased downward Fisher-Yates (i:n-1→1, j=lcg_int(s,i+1)∈[0,i] inclusive — NOT the biased [0,n) variant); (4) the bounded `lcg_int = pos % n` modulo bias over pos∈[0,2^63) is < 1e-16 rel for any realistic n (n=1000→1.08e-16; only material as n→2^63) = NEGLIGIBLE, standard pragmatic tradeoff; (5) `surface_code._rng_u` is a self-consistent Knuth LCG (mislabeled "splitmix64-style" but claims only internal byte-repro for a selftest seal, which it has). 🟠 FLAGGED (off-flagship-path, NOT blocked): `rng.hexa::randn_box_muller`/`randn_matrix` Box-Muller GAUSSIAN uses platform libm log/cos/sin (sqrt is IEEE-exact) → 1-2 ULP Darwin vs glibc divergence (F-OP33/OP66 class), so the gaussian stream is same-machine-deterministic but NOT bit-exact cross-platform; it is OFF the flame bit-exact training path (flame inits via the uniform dt_lcg, not Box-Muller; consumers = optim/cpgd init + chem/vina docking only). NOT moved off libm (@D stdlib_trig_libm mandates libm trig); instead the module header's overclaim of "deterministic" was corrected with an honest cross-platform REPRODUCIBILITY caveat. REGRESSION LOCK `stdlib/sim_universe/anu_stream/xs64s_reference_test.hexa` (inlines the pair-arithmetic + xs64s_step w/ the CORRECTED constant, pins the full (hi,lo) 64-bit output for seed (1,1) vs the canonical xorshift64* vector): RUN PASS 10/10; negative control (revert to 625058449) FAILS at hi[0]/hi[2..4] (5/10) = discriminating. CLOSURE/FLAGS: client_example.hexa is a standalone Monte-Carlo π demo imported by NOTHING in the build closure (grep=0) → closure-OUT, ZERO byte-eq/self-host-fixpoint impact; rng.hexa edit is a header comment only; net-additive (1 constant char-pair + 2 comment corrections + 1 new test) → wipe_guard N/A. ONE HONEST LINE: seeded PRNGs ARE reproducible + unbiased on the integer/uniform path (core NR LCG + flame training LCGs reproduce their documented sequences byte-exactly cross-platform, Fisher-Yates correct, bounded bias <1e-16) — the one WRONG-SEQUENCE bug (xorshift64* demo multiplier hi word) is fixed and reference-locked; only the Box-Muller gaussian is same-machine-only (libm), now documented not overclaimed. - [x] **OP-137 — complete OP-134 residual #1: bare native `#{}` map signed-zero hash↔equality fix at the C-runtime root (🔴→🟢, KEY-path only, display "-0.0" preserved).** OP-134 fixed signed-zero LOST-KEY in stdlib HashSet/counter (pure-hexa boundary normaliser) but FLAGGED that the bare native `#{}` map carries the SAME root in the GUARDED C runtime — the map keys on a C `char*` produced by `hexa_to_cstring(key)` (FNV-1a hashed + strcmp-compared, internally consistent by construction), and `hexa_to_cstring` has TWO float branches (Val-struct fast path + HexaVal fast path) that `snprintf("%.1f", f)` a whole-valued key: `%.1f` of `-0.0` emits `"-0.0"` ≠ `"0.0"`, so `m[-0.0]` hashes to a DIFFERENT slot than `m[0.0]` → lost key / silent duplicate. `hexa_to_cstring` is KEY-ONLY (callers = `hexa_map_get_v`/`hexa_map_set_v` + the `m[k]` get/set codegen sites); DISPLAY uses a SEPARATE function (`hexa_to_string`/`_hexa_to_string_rec`/`__hexa_format_float_for_print`). SOURCE OF TRUTH: `runtime_core.c` is a gitignored build artifact regenerated from the SSOT emitter `self/runtime_core_emit.hexa` (the surface OP-132 edited), so the real edit is the emitted-C STRING for `hexa_to_cstring`, not the generated `.c`. FIX (net-additive): `if (f == 0.0) f = 0.0;` before EACH of the two float snprintf branches in the emitter (`f == 0.0` is TRUE for both ±0.0; the `0.0` literal clears the sign bit → +0.0). REPRODUCE (shipping stale-frozen self-host CLI, g5-honest): `let m=#{}; m[0.0]=1; has_key(m, 0.0*-1.0)` → **false (LOST KEY)** while `str(-0.0)` → **"-0.0"** (display already correct). VERIFY (generated-C level + discriminating test — the deployed binary is stale-frozen so, exactly like OP-132/OP-134, the proof is at the emitter/generated-C level): re-ran `hexa run self/runtime_core_emit.hexa /tmp/op137_runtime_core.c` → both `if (f == 0.0) f = 0.0;` appear INSIDE `hexa_to_cstring` before `%.1f` and before `__hexa_format_float_mode` (generated-C L1596 + L1614), and ZERO OP-137 markers in any display function (total markers == 2, both key-path); self-contained C harness: BEFORE `key(0.0)="0.0"` vs `key(-0.0)="-0.0"`=LOST, AFTER both `"0.0"`=match, DISPLAY `str(-0.0)="-0.0"` preserved. REGRESSION LOCK `test/native_map_signed_zero_smoke.hexa` (12 checks: signed-zero key round-trip has_key/read/overwrite/symmetry + non-zero floats stay distinct + int key untouched + display asserts `str(-0.0)=="-0.0"` & `str(0.0)=="0.0"`); DISCRIMINATING — on the stale PRE-fix runtime the 6 key-match checks FAIL (`map key '-0.0' not found`) while the 2 display asserts already PASS (`6 passed, 6 failed`); after the emitter fix + a from-scratch runtime rebuild all 12 pass. CLOSURE: `self/runtime_core_emit.hexa` IS in the build_selfhost closure but the change is an emitted-C string literal (like OP-132) so the self-emit byte-eq fixpoint re-runs identically; selfhost-gates-summary gate status recorded on the PR (if the fixpoint breaks → 🟠 DEFERRED for a build-host, NOT force-merged). WIPE-GUARD: tiny net-additive (2 lines + comments in the emitter), 0 deletions. SCOPE FENCE: KEY path only — non-zero floats/ints/strings/NaN/±inf untouched; cross-type int/float (1 vs 1.0) and NaN-distinct-identity remain pre-existing P1/inverse limits (OP-134), NOT regressed. ONE HONEST LINE: the bare `#{}` map now hashes `-0.0 == 0.0` to the same slot WHILE display still prints `"-0.0"` (Python-exact: `hash(-0.0)==hash(0.0)` yet `str(-0.0)=="-0.0"`). - [x] **OP-134 — LANE-1 hash↔equality contract consistency audit: 🔴 1 signed-zero LOST-KEY violation FIXED + 🟢 CONVERGED (3 containers ×8 key-classes audited / 1 reachable violation / 2 stdlib surfaces fixed).** Contract: `a == b` ⟹ they MUST map to the SAME backing key, else a hashset/map silently LOSES or DUPLICATES entries. ARCHITECTURE: hexa's native map (`#{}` · `m[k]=v` · `has_key` · `keys`) is keyed on a C `char*` — FNV-1a hashed (`hexa_fnv1a_str`) + `strcmp`-compared (`hmap_find`, runtime_core.c:2565); hash AND eq are over the SAME key bytes → INTERNALLY CONSISTENT BY CONSTRUCTION, so the entire contract reduces to "equal values MUST stringify to the same key". stdlib HashSet/counter key via `to_string(item)`; the native map via `hexa_to_cstring(key)` (the `m[5]==m["5"]` path). MATRIX (key→eq→keystr→consistent): int "%lld" ✓ · string identity ✓ · bool true/false ✓ · non-zero float %.1f/%g ✓ · **+0.0/-0.0 → "0.0" vs "-0.0" 🔴** · NaN → "NaN" (all NaNs collapse, collision=allowed-inverse 🟠) · ±inf ✓ · int1/float1.0 → "1"/"1.0" (pre-existing documented P1 cross-type 🟠). 🔴 VIOLATION: IEEE `-0.0 == 0.0` is TRUE but a runtime-computed `-0.0` (e.g. `0.0*-1.0`, gradients, `-1.0/inf`) stringifies to `"-0.0"` ≠ `"0.0"`. REPRODUCED on the shipping self-hosted CLI verbatim — HashSet: insert `0.0` then `contains(-0.0)` → **false (LOST KEY)**, `insert(-0.0)` → **true (DUPLICATE)**, `len` → **2** (should be true/false/1); the bare native `#{}` map has the same `has_key(m, -0.0)`→false. Real consequence: any set/map keyed on a float reachable to -0.0 silently loses/duplicates entries. FIX (LANE-1 stdlib scope — pure hexa, no C/self-host edit): a zero-collapse key-normaliser at the container boundary — `_hashset_norm`/`_counter_norm`: for a float `== 0.0` return `0.0 + 0.0` (IEEE round-to-nearest CLEARS the sign bit → +0.0); ints/strings/NaN/±inf/non-zero floats pass through untouched. Applied in `stdlib/hashset.hexa` (insert/remove/contains) + `stdlib/alloc/collections.hexa` (`counter()`). Matches how `==` already treats signed zero AND Python (`str(-0.0)=="-0.0"` yet `hash(-0.0)==hash(0.0)`); display `to_string` UNCHANGED so -0.0 still prints "-0.0" and OP-132 float round-trip fidelity is intact (KEY-PATH ONLY). VERIFY (g5 honest, shipping self-hosted CLI, AOT cache busted, real `use "stdlib/hashset"` path): `test/hashset_smoke.hexa` 73 passed / 0 failed `__HASHSET_SMOKE__ PASS`, incl. 10 NEW T17 OP-134 checks all PASS (contains(-0.0)-after-insert(0.0), -0.0-literal, re-insert-not-new, len-stays-1 both directions, non-zero floats stay distinct, int key untouched); the same test FAILS pre-fix (contains→false, len→2) = discriminating regression guard. 🟠 FLAGGED RESIDUALS (out of LANE-1 stdlib scope — handoff): (1) the bare native `#{}` map carries the SAME -0.0 root in the guarded C runtime — `hexa_to_cstring` BOTH float branches (runtime_core.c ~L1587 valstruct + ~L1601 HexaVal, `%.1f` of -0.0 → "-0.0"); fix = `if (f==0.0) f=0.0;` before each snprintf (key-path only, NOT `_hexa_to_string_rec` display), gated by self-host byte-eq + wipe/diff guards → deferred to a self/ closure PR; (2) NaN-key collision left deterministic-not-corrupt (string-keyed model limit, documented in `_hashset_norm`); (3) `self/rt/array_ops.hexa` group_by/frequencies key on to_string too (self-host closure — flagged not edited). CLOSURE/FLAGS: both edited files are stdlib LEAVES — `compiler/main.hexa` never imports them (build_selfhost closure-OUT) → ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact, no selfhost gate semantics changed; net-additive (2 normalisers + 4 reroutes + 10 asserts, 0 deletions) → wipe_guard N/A; NO .tape. ONE HONEST LINE: int/string/bool/non-zero-float keys were already consistent by construction; the ONE real reachable violation was signed-zero (`-0.0` lost a `0.0` key) — now FIXED in stdlib HashSet/counter so they can no longer silently lose/duplicate a signed-zero key (bare `#{}` root handed off to the C-runtime closure). LANE-1. $0 · 0-pod · NO GPU · no vast · no foreign-pod · leak-0. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP134-HASH-EQ-CONTRACT.txt.

- [x] **OP-132 — LANE-1 float→string round-trip / shortest-repr correctness audit: 🔴 1 ROUND-TRIP-LOSSY default-formatter class FIXED + 🟢 CONVERGED (3 paths audited / 1 lossy default (4 emit sites) / 4 sites fixed).** The OP-37/40 const-fold work fixed the PARSE direction (string→float literal); OP-132 audits the FORMAT direction (float→string). PROPERTY (A) round-trip exactness `parse_float(to_string(d)) == d` bit-identical; (B) shortest/clean repr (Python repr semantics). ROOT CAUSE (🔴): the DEFAULT float→string format was `"%g"` (6 significant digits) in EVERY non-integer float branch of the C runtime — emitted by the SSOT `self/runtime_core_emit.hexa` (`self/runtime_core.c` is a gitignored build artifact regenerated from it). 4 emit sites shared the lossy default: `hexa_to_cstring` Val-struct + HexaVal fast paths, `_hexa_to_string_rec` TAG_FLOAT (no-stdlib build), and the `hexa_to_string` non-integer branch (stdlib build), plus the shared `__hexa_format_float_for_print` print path. `%g`/6-digits TRUNCATES: `1.0/3.0`→`"0.333333"` reparses to a DIFFERENT double; `2^53+2`→`"9.0072e+15"`, `pi`→`"3.14159"`, `DBL_MAX`→`"1.79769e+308"`, `123456789.123456789`→`"1.23457e+08"`, `0.30000000000000004`→`"0.3"` all LOSE precision — silent data loss on any round-trip (the g15/g17 env opt-ins existed but the DEFAULT every program got was lossy). Also audited & CLEAN: `stdlib/runtime/numeric.hexa` `rt_to_string_scalar`→`rt_format_float_f(f,1)` is reached ONLY for INTEGER-valued floats in the live build (C inline guard handles non-integer first) → `"3.0"`, correct, left as-is. FIX (minimal, net-additive, in the emitter SSOT): replaced the single `"%g"` default with a 4-mode selector via `HEXA_FLOAT_REPR` — REPR_SHORTEST (NEW DEFAULT) = smallest `%.{p}g` p∈[1,17] that reparses EXACTLY (strtod = IEEE-correct inverse; memcpy bit-compare guards -0.0) = round-trip-SAFE AND shortest = Python repr(); REPR_G17/G15 fixed-precision escape hatches; REPR_G6 = legacy lossy `%g` for golden-snapshot stability. All 4 emit sites route through new `__hexa_format_float_mode()`; the integer-valued `%.1f` convention (trk.27: `3.0`→`"3.0"`) + NaN/inf canonical casing (PROBE r14-E) UNCHANGED. VERIFICATION: emitter regenerated (`hexa run self/runtime_core_emit.hexa` → runtime_core.c 405,596 B exit 0); the EXACT generated `__hexa_format_float_mode` extracted + COMPILED clean (clang -Wall) + run → DEFAULT shortest **0/21 ROUND-TRIP-LOSSY** (was 7/21 under `%g`); g17 0/21; g6 7/21 (preserved opt-out). Shortest digits match Python repr() verbatim (one-third→`0.3333333333333333`, pi→`3.141592653589793`, `0.30000000000000004` exact). PRE-FIX live repro (stale Jun-7 hexa, old `%g`): self-test PASS 18 / FAIL 8 (round-trip lost on one-third, pi, 0.0001, 123456789…, DBL_MAX + shortest collapse 0.3…04→"0.3"). FINDING (Δ): default float→string 7/21 lossy (silent data loss) → 0/21 + shortest repr. LOCK: NEW `self/test_float_roundtrip.hexa` — 14 round-trip-exactness asserts (incl. all 6 previously-lossy doubles) + 6 shortest-repr + 3 whole-float convention + 3 special-value (inf/-inf/NaN); FAILS 8 on stale binary (deployment-lag canary), GREEN under regenerated runtime. CLOSURE/FLAGS: `runtime_core_emit.hexa` IS the runtime SSOT and IS in the build_selfhost closure; edit is NET-ADDITIVE (mode selector + 4 reroutes, 0 net deletions) → wipe_guard N/A; the change is to EMITTED C-runtime string literals (not codegen/parse), so the self-emit byte-eq fixpoint re-runs identically (same emitter every stage). HONEST RESIDUAL: the from-scratch self-emit fixpoint ladder (~3.5h, build-host, NOT 0-pod) is NOT re-run here — fix PROVEN at emit level (generated C compiles + runs + 0/21 lossy); deployed `hexa run` serves old `%g` until a runtime re-promote (stale-oracle trap; source correct). NO .tape. ONE HONEST LINE: hexa's default float→string was round-trip-LOSSY (`%g` 6 digits silently truncated ~1/3 of typical doubles); it now emits the shortest string that reparses bit-exactly (Python repr semantics) — round-trip-SAFE by default, with g17/g15/g6 env escape hatches. - [x] **OP-128 — byte-level-BPE tokenizer round-trip (the `qwen_unmap_bytes` path OP-127 FLAGGED latent): 🔴 FIXED ×2 distinct bug classes in `self/ml/qwen_bpe.hexa`.** OP-127 flagged `qwen_unmap_bytes` as a distinct latent detokenize path it did not audit. OP-128 audited the whole byte-level-BPE encode/decode of the production Qwen tokenizer and found BOTH classic bugs. **(a) BIJECTION not exact vs GPT-2/HF:** `qwen_build_byte_to_str` mapped each non-printable byte to `256 + i` (per-byte offset) instead of the canonical GPT-2 RUNNING COUNTER `256 + n`. The two agree only for bytes 0..32 and DIVERGE for the 35 non-printable bytes ≥127 (0x7F, 0x80..0xA0, 0xAD) — 34 of which (0x80..0xA0, 0xAD) are UTF-8 CONTINUATION bytes present in EVERY Korean/CJK/emoji char, so the wrong visible glyph never matched Qwen's vocab.json keys: encode produced wrong/missing tokens for ALL non-Latin multibyte text + decode of HF tokens carrying U+0122..U+0142 mojibaked. Internal decode∘encode was self-consistent (same wrong table both ways) which MASKED it — only interop with a real HF vocab exposes it. **This is the EXACT defect OP-28b already cured in the `tokenizer_bpe.hexa`/op28b twin; `qwen_bpe.hexa` was the un-fixed copy.** FIX = running-counter `256 + n` → Python-port oracle vs openai/HF `bytes_to_unicode`: mismatches 35→0, '가'/'한글'/'中文'/'😀' encode against HF vocab missing-bytes []. **(b) DETOKENIZE corrupts multibyte glyphs (even within ONE token):** `qwen_unmap_bytes` had two `from_char_code`-over-raw-byte misuses (the OP-127 class): it rebuilt the glyph key per byte via `from_char_code(char_code(tok,j))` (re-UTF-8-encodes a raw byte ≥128 → 2-byte glyph becomes 4-byte blob, str_to_byte MISS) AND emitted the recovered raw byte via `from_char_code(byte_val)` (byte 0xEA → 2-byte U+00EA, not the single raw byte). FIX (matching the proven `tokenizer_bpe.hexa::bpe_decode` twin) = size glyph by lead byte (192/224/240→2/3/4), extract via `.slice` (raw-byte preserving), emit via `chr(byte_val)` (single raw byte), fall back to the raw slice on a miss. Cross-token correctness then automatic: `out` is a raw-byte buffer + `qwen_bpe_decode` concatenates per-token unmaps, so a multibyte char whose UTF-8 bytes span 2+ tokens reassembles BEFORE decode. PROOF on local interp: BEFORE unmap(2-byte glyph)=4 bytes b0=195 (key 'Ä·' not found); AFTER unmap(한 packed)=[237,149,156] (correct UTF-8). LOCK: NEW `test/t54_qwen_bpe_multibyte_roundtrip.hexa` (`hexa run` → 16/16 PASS): 256 bijection distinct+inverse, canonical-codepoint spot-checks on the broken bytes (0x7F→U+0121, 0x80→U+0122, 0xA0→U+0142, NOT buggy U+0180), and the CRITICAL multibyte CROSS-TOKEN cases — Korean 3-byte char split 1+1+1 across 3 tokens, 4-byte emoji split across 2/3 tokens, mixed ASCII+multibyte — all reassemble byte-exact. `hexa parse` clean ×2. CLOSURE: `self/ml/qwen_bpe.hexa` imported ONLY by tests + `serve_alm.hexa`, never `compiler/main.hexa`/`tool/hexa_ld.hexa` → build_selfhost-closure-OUT → ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact, NO selfhost gate; t54 is a closure-OUT leaf test. FLAGS: 1 source edit closure-OUT; net-additive/substituting (no >50-line deletion) → wipe_guard N/A; NO .tape. HONEST RESIDUAL: verified by Python-port-of-source oracle + pure-hexa interpreter test; the frozen Jun-7 binary carries the fix only after a rebuild (source correct — stale-oracle trap). The earlier t54 FAIL was a stale-interp nested-struct-literal-in-helper SCOPING quirk (not a logic bug); hoisting the QwenBpe value → 16/16 GREEN. Byte-level-BPE detokenize is NOW correct for multibyte cross-token text. $0 · 0-pod · NO GPU · no vast · no foreign-pod · leak-0. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP128-BYTELEVEL-BPE-ROUNDTRIP.txt.
- [x] **OP-131 — LANE-1 date/time arithmetic & parsing value-correctness audit (promotes OP-93's deferred 🟠 civil field-range flag): 🔴 1 silent-normalisation bug FIXED + 🟢 CONVERGED (8 helpers audited / 1 buggy / 1 fixed).** OP-93 flagged that the `civil` date helper does NOT validate field ranges — left as a 🟠 design note. PROMOTED to a real audit: tested every date/time/calendar helper's edge inputs against a Python `datetime`/`calendar` oracle. CLEAN (value-correct, bit-exact, max|Δ|=0): `stdlib/time/civil.hexa` Howard-Hinnant `civil_from_days`/`days_from_civil` (proleptic Gregorian over leap 2000/2024, non-leap-century 1900, negative epoch 1969-12-31→-86400, Dec-31→Jan-1 boundary) + `iso8601_format_native`/`rfc3339_*`; `self/runtime/datetime_mini_pure.hexa` `is_leap_year_pure`/`days_in_month_pure`/`day_of_year_pure`/`days_between_pure` (all DOCUMENT "no validity check — caller's responsibility" AND no in-repo caller violates it → precondition, not a bug) + `weekday_zeller_pure` (OP-81 locked-clean leaf, not re-audited); `self/std_time.hexa` `is_leap`/`parts_to_timestamp`/`timestamp_to_parts` (round-trip-correct for valid fields). THE BUG (🔴): `iso8601_parse_native` — the public input boundary — documents "Returns -1 on a malformed input" but NEVER range-checked the fields, so EVERY invalid date was silently normalised through `days_from_civil` into a GARBAGE epoch instead of the -1 sentinel: `"2024-02-30"`→1709251200 (=2024-03-01, Python raises ValueError), `"2024-13-05"`→2025-01-05, month 0, day 0/32, hour 25, minute 60, AND `"1900-02-29"`→-2203891200 (1900 is NOT leap: ÷100 not ÷400) all accepted. A user round-tripping an invalid date had it silently corrupted. FIX (minimal): added `month∈[1,12]`, `hour≤23`, `minute≤59`, `second≤60` (leap-second), `day∈[1,_days_in_month(y,m)]` checks (new leap-aware `_days_in_month` helper) before `days_from_civil`, returning the already-documented -1. VALID edge cases UNCHANGED & byte-exact vs oracle: 2024-02-29 leap day→1709208000, 2000-02-29 (÷400)→951782400, 2016-12-31T23:59:60 leap-sec accepted, epoch0→0, roundtrip(fmt(1.7e9))→1700000000. LOCK: `stdlib/time/civil_test.hexa` (@ci_gate) +11 asserts (8 `rej_*` invalid→-1 each previously a silent garbage epoch · 3 `ok_*` valid-edge→exact oracle epoch); `hexa run` patched source → civil self-test 21/21 PASS; both files `hexa parse` clean. HONEST STALE-ORACLE RESIDUAL: `hexa run` resolves `use "stdlib/time/civil"` against the INSTALLED (`~/.hx/src`) frozen stdlib, so the deployed binary still shows the OLD garbage epochs until the stdlib re-ships — NOT a logic bug; the fix LOGIC is proven by interpreting the patched source directly (`use "/tmp/civil_patched"` → all 13 edge cases PASS). CLOSURE: `civil.hexa` is a stdlib leaf — `compiler/main.hexa` never imports it (build_selfhost closure-OUT) → ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact, no selfhost gate semantics changed; net-additive (validation block + 1 helper + 11 asserts, 0 deletions) → wipe_guard N/A; NO .tape. ONE HONEST LINE: every date/time ARITHMETIC helper is value-correct; the single real bug was the ISO-8601 PARSER silently normalising invalid fields instead of rejecting them. - [x] **OP-130 — LANE-1 repo-wide sweep for the OP-129 NARROW error-mask class (build/CI gates that silently swallow a real failure): 🔴×3 fragile diagnostic masks FIXED + 🟢 census CONVERGED.** OP-129 found `build_selfhost.sh`'s `grep -iE 'error:'` mask MISSED clang's "Undefined symbols for architecture …" linker error (no `error:` token) → empty diagnostic before the artifact-existence `fail`. OP-130 censuses EVERY error/success-detection gate across tool/*.sh (≈110) · .githooks/* · .github/workflows/*.yml: `grep -i*error`, `grep 'error:'`, `| grep -q`, `grep -c`, `if … grep`, `&& echo PASS`/`|| echo FAIL`, CI `::error::`. CLASSIFICATION: the vast majority are ROBUST — pass/fail is decided by EXIT CODE / ARTIFACT-EXISTENCE (`rc=$?`, `[ -s rt.o ] || fail`, `[ -x SMB ] || exit`), with grep used only as a 2nd banner-smoke check or a count METRIC; CI yml gates on step exit codes + `::error::` annotations, never on output-grep magic words; the dispatch_*.sh `grep -q READY/OK` are retried remote-readiness probes; the one grep-RESULT-gates-passfail site (`build_aprime.sh:204` `[ -n "$CL_ERR" ]`) is OR'd with `[ ! -x "$OUT" ]` so a no-`error:`-token clang failure still fails via the missing artifact. The ONLY surviving offenders = 3 narrow `error:`-only DIAGNOSTIC masks that OP-129's targeted fix didn't reach (its #3296 merge consolidated the hexat TU so that 4th narrow mask is now gone): `build_selfhost.sh:173` (rt.o clang) + `build_aprime.sh:222` (rt.o clang) + `build_aprime.sh:223` (smoke .s clang). These are "diagnostic-fragile": the GATE never silently passed (artifact backstop), but the narrow mask SWALLOWED the real linker/assembler text (`Undefined symbols`/`ld:`/`fatal error:`/`cannot find`), leaving a blank diagnostic before the failure — the confusing-failure half of the OP-129 class. FIX (3 lines, matches OP-129's widening): all three → `grep -iE 'error:|undefined|ld:|fatal|cannot find'`. Demonstrated missed mode: a smoke-.s `fatal error: cannot find …` emits NO `error:`-matching line → operator sees blank then "smoke link failed" with the cause hidden; widened mask surfaces it verbatim. Widening a DISPLAY-only grep can only print MORE real failure text → ZERO false-positive / CI-break hazard (the gate is the artifact/exit check, not the grep). `bash -n` clean on both; `grep -rn "grep -iE 'error:'" tool/*.sh` → 0 narrow masks remain repo-wide. FLAGS: 2 BUILD SCRIPTS (`.sh`) only, NO compiler source → ZERO byte-eq/fixpoint impact, no selfhost gate semantics changed; net-additive (3 widened patterns, 0 deletions) → wipe_guard N/A; NO .tape. HONEST RESIDUAL: no known case remains where a NARROW grep DECIDES pass/fail — every grep gate is exit-code/artifact-backstopped or a count metric alongside an exit check; the silent-swallow class is CONVERGED. - [x] **OP-123 — LANE-2 malformed binary CONTAINER / media / archive parser robustness sweep (CWE-190 int-overflow → CWE-125/787 OOB / DoS): 🔴×1 FIXED + 🟢 CONVERGED census.** Hunted (magic)+repeated-(len/offset, payload) walkers that TRUST a declared chunk length/count/offset from untrusted bytes then read/slice past the buffer, loop unboundedly, or WRAP on the length/offset arithmetic (hexa int = 64-bit SIGNED, wrap real — OP-118/121). EXCLUDED per fences: net/HTTP (OP-122 lane), crypto/TLS/x509/asn1 (OP-105/118), JSON recursion (OP-120). 🔴 FINDING — `stdlib/ckpt/verifier.hexa` `verify_pcc` (the .pcc proof-carrying-checkpoint header parser): reads chunk_count:u64 / payload_len:u64 / chunk_size:u32 / cert_block_len:u32 from an untrusted file then forms the layout via RAW MULTIPLICATION (`chunk_count * pcc_dir_entry_size()` (*20), `chunk_count * chunk_size`, `payload_start + payload_len`) and gates on the SINGLE primary guard `expected_total == total_bytes`. A crafted **chunk_count = 4392081922311798020 (~2^62.4)** makes `chunk_count*20` OVERFLOW → wraps to a NEGATIVE dir_size → `payload_start = -6.1e18` (nonsense), `payload_end = 176`, `expected_total = 200` — a 200-byte crafted file PASSES the structural guard (DEFEATED by the wrap). Downstream the wrapped negative payload_start makes the per-chunk bounds checks (`offset < payload_start`, `+len > payload_end`) NO-OPS (any offset≥0 < a negative payload_start), and the dir walk `while ci < chunk_count` (bound = the 4.4e18 count) can index `buf` OOB. RUNTIME OOB SEMANTICS (probed verbatim `hexa run`): `buf[100]` on len-3 → HARD FAULT `index 100 out of bounds (len 3)` (process abort), NOT a silent garbage read → the wrap yields either a graceful-but-WRONG reject or an UNCONTROLLED ABORT (DoS — verify_pcc's whole contract is to return a tamper-code DICT, never crash). FIX (defense-in-depth, fail-closed, surgical): every declared region (cert block, chunk directory, payload) is a SUBSET of the file → bound each field against total_bytes BEFORE any multiplication — `chunk_count <= total_bytes / DIR_ENTRY_SIZE` (wrap-free DIVISION), `chunk_size`/`payload_len`/`cert_block_len <= total_bytes`; every subsequent product is then far under 2^62 → the layout arithmetic CANNOT wrap and the structural guard is no longer bypassable. CENSUS REST 🟢 CLEAN (honest g5, nothing invented): `stdlib/safetensors.hexa` safetensors_read ALREADY hardened (`header_len<=0` · `8+header_len>n` · per-tensor `s>=0 && e>=s && data_start+e<=n` before every slice); `stdlib/wasm/wasm_leb128` *_decode loop `while i<n` (buffer-bounded, no OOB) + doc'd @pure scaffold with ZERO alloc/offset-sink callers (same disposition OP-118); `wasm_section_header/_type_section/_export` EMIT-ONLY; `ckpt/merkle`+`chunk_store` operate on in-memory leaf arrays / program-owned data NOT a wire stream. <!-- ANCHOR:OP-118-INT-OVERFLOW-SIZE-ARITH (unique anchor — LANE-2 0-pod CWE-190→CWE-125/787 size-COMPUTATION-step class = a binary/protocol/crypto PARSER reads a length/count FIELD from untrusted bytes then does ARITHMETIC (accumulate a big-endian width, add a header, derive der_next) to compute a SIZE/OFFSET/COPY-COUNT; if that arithmetic WRAPS past the int domain (→ negative/garbage) or a parsed length is treated as SIGNED, the computed size is WRONG. The size-computation complement of OP-105 (offset-OOB) and OP-115 (unbounded-work). SEMANTICS PROBE (verbatim `hexa run`): hexa `int` is 64-bit SIGNED (i63 effective) — `imax+1=-9223372036854775808` (WRAPS not bignum), `4e9*4e9=-2446744073709551616`, `neg<cap PASSED bound (neg=-5)` (negative length passes `< cap`), `total=x+y` wraps below x so `total<x?=true` (bypass). All 4 sub-classes REAL; overflow is only reachable where the declared width is VARIABLE (DER long-form up to 127 bytes) or full-64-bit (websocket 127-form) — narrow ≤24/32-bit fields are wrap-IMMUNE. VERDICT 🟢 class CLOSED — 🔴×2 FIXED+locked. 🔴#1 crypto/asn1_der der_value_len: long-form `nbytes=n&0x7F` (≤127) then `v=(v<<8)|byte` with NO cap → ≥9-byte (or 8-byte high-bit) length WRAPS negative; der_next=value_start+v REGRESSES below value_start (TLV iteration loops backward) + n_len/e_len/r_len/s_len go negative into x509_{min,rsa,p256,validity}. REPRO `hexa run`: 9-byte-FF prefix=-1 fixed=0, 8-byte-FF prefix=-1 fixed=0, der_next pre=10<start=11→fixed=11. FIX: `if nbytes>8 {return 0}` + `if v<0 {return 0}` (return 0=empty value, the graceful short-read result a truncated buffer already yields). REPRO: top-bit-set prefix=-9223372036854775808 fixed=-1. FIX: `if val<0 {return ""}`. LOCK stdlib/net/websocket_native_len64_overflow_test.hexa __HEXA_OP118_WS_LEN64_OVERFLOW__ PASS 5/5 (valid 300/65536/2^53 preserved · top-bit+all-FF rejected). CENSUS rest 🟢 CLEAN (honest g5): every TLS13/X.509 fixed-width length is ≤24-bit (alpn/cert_auth/key_share/supported_groups/sig_algs 16-bit `end=2+list_len` co-bounded by `i<len`; tls13_certificate uint24 sums; new_session_ticket/early_data u32 values) so `+`/`*` CANNOT wrap i63; der_int/der_oid_str=value-decode not size-sink; x509_rsa exponent=value; hkdf length=trusted RFC param (neg→loop exits); poly1305/sha512/ripemd160/scrypt=masked 32-bit crypto words; record_aad/traffic_key=local-sender sizes. A counted repeat expands in `_re_run_repeat` to `n` MANDATORY matcher iterations, EACH calling `_re_repeat_marker` → `nodes.push([12,...])` — so the parsed count is BOTH the loop bound AND the allocation count. A malicious 11-byte pattern `a{999999999}` forces ~1e9 iterations + ~1e9 array growths = multi-GB allocation + near-infinite loop (CWE-1333 ReDoS / CWE-400 uncontrolled resource consumption). VERBATIM REPRO (hermetic leaf oracle, `hexa run` 0.1.0-dispatch, verbatim-inlined `_re_parse_repeat` count-accumulate + `_re_run_repeat`): `parsed count from a{999999999} = 999999999` / `a{1000000} → matcher iterations = 1000000` / `a{999999999} → iterations EXCEED 5,000,000 sentinel; real code would run 999999999 iters + push that many marker nodes`. FIX (surgical, fail-closed, ZERO valid-semantics change): `@pure fn _RE_MAX_REPEAT() -> int { return 100000 }` + in both the lo- and hi-accumulate loops `if lo/hi > _RE_MAX_REPEAT() { p.ok = false; return -1 }` — over-limit counts FAIL the parse (all three entry points reject via their existing `!p.ok` guard) rather than silently truncating to a wrong count. Cap 100000 is far above any realistic stdlib/config/lint pattern (RE2 caps at 1000, Java/.NET/Python at small bounds) yet bounds worst-case work. `{n,}` unbounded (hi=-1) still allowed (mandatory part `lo` capped; open tail bounded by input length via the progress guard). VERBATIM PROOF (post-fix leaf oracle of the PATCHED parse): a{999999999}/a{999999999,}/a{0,999999999} → REJECTED (match=false, no alloc/loop); a{3}/a{2,4}/a{100000}(=cap) → PARSE-OK; a{100001}(>cap) → REJECTED — guard fires BEFORE any expansion. <!-- ANCHOR:OP-112-NAN-INF-SWEEP (unique anchor — LANE-1 0-pod: SYSTEMIC silent-NaN/Inf-propagation class census across stdlib numeric/scientific (NON-crypto = LANE-2), generalizing OP-110. Swept log/ln(x≤0), sqrt(x<0 from roundoff/variance/1-x²), pow(x<0 frac-y), division-by-nonempty-zero, and re-confirmed every acos/asin. 🔴 ONE genuine bug → FIXED, exactly the OP-110 shape and the very site OP-110 had flagged 🟠 "NOT reproducible": stdlib/kernels/solar/solar_kernel.hexa `ephemeris` (pub; solar_elevation/solar_zenith/apparent_zenith build on it) fed the elevation SINE `cosφcosδcosH + sinφsinδ` STRAIGHT to the libm `asin` builtin with NO clamp. That sum-of-products = cos(φ−δ) at H=0, ≤1 by Cauchy–Schwarz (intended result finite), but the fp sum overshoots 1 by ~1e-16 (1 ULP) for plausible inputs — latitude ≈ declination, i.e. the sun overhead at solar noon. libm asin (unlike rt_asin which clamps |x|>1→±π/2) returns a SILENT NaN that propagates into elevation AND zenith=90−elevation. OP-110 called this 🟠 "theoretically possible only at the exact zenith pole, NOT reproducible"; OP-112 MADE IT REPRODUCIBLE — a 900-pt lat=dec sweep (`hexa run`) → `UNGUARDED asin(>1) NaN count = 35` (35/900 plausible inputs silent-NaN), exact overshoot `asin(1.0000000000000002) is_nan = true`. PYTHON cross-check (IEEE-754): max arg over the lat,dec sweep = 1.0000000000000002 at lat=dec=−15.6°, math.asin(that)=NaN, clamp→1.0→asin=π/2. The declination-sine asin (sinε·sinλ = product of two |sin|≤1 → ≤1 structurally) left unwrapped — clamping it is a no-op; only the genuine sum-of-products site touched; no contract change (no-op for every in-domain input). <!-- ANCHOR:OP-109-CRYPTO-ENCODE-VERIFY-OOB (unique anchor — LANE-2 0-pod: close OP-107's flagged verify-side OOB + audit the crypto/TLS ENCODE side (inverse of OP-105's parse-side OOB sweep). VERBATIM REPRO via `hexa run`: a short sig (len 10) into ed25519_verify's first loop `sig[i] i<32` → `index 10 out of bounds (len 10)` (process abort = DoS); a short qx (len 4) into p256's `_be32_to_limbs` `b[30]/b[31]` → `index 30 out of bounds (len 4)`. (Defense-in-depth, NOT forgery — all in-tree x509_* callers already guard.) FIX (mirror OP-104/105 alpn guard): ed25519_verify prologue `if len(pubk)!=32 || len(sig)!=64 {return false}` (RFC 8032); p256_verify prologue `if len(qx)!=32||len(qy)!=32||len(hash)!=32||len(r)!=32||len(s)!=32 {return false}`. PART B (encode-side length-field audit): census of the SERIALIZERS that BUILD length-prefixed wire. 🔴 ONE REAL BUG FOUND + FIXED — the tls13 RECORD-AAD length (tls13_record_aad caller in tls13_client_record_io.hexa): RFC 8446 §5.2 requires the AAD length field = TLSCiphertext.length = encrypted_record length = |TLSInnerPlaintext|+16(AEAD tag). The seal caller passed `len(plain)` (16 SHORT) and the open caller `body_len-16` (also short) — SELF-CONSISTENT so hexa↔hexa round-trip worked, but BOTH WRONG vs the wire: differential vs RFC 8448 §3 documented record header `17 03 03 02 a2` (len 0x02a2) — hexa emitted 0x0292 (16 short) → a CONFORMING peer (OpenSSL/BoringSSL) recomputes the right AAD and the AEAD tag verify FAILS = real interop break. FIX: seal passes `len(plain)+16`, open passes `body_len`; seal-AAD==open-AAD preserved (hexa↔hexa still round-trips) AND now == on-wire record-header length == RFC value. 🟢 CLEAN (length field == body, round-trip == identity, verified): tls13_ext_build (uint16 == len(value); tls13_ext_next/ext_length round-trip; supported_versions wire `00 2b 00 03 02 03 04`), tls13_client_hello_body (session_id uint8, cipher_suites uint16 csn*2, extensions uint16 — each == its body), _keyshare_entry/key_share client wrap (uint16 key_len, shares-vec, ext-len each == body; X25519 key_len 0x0020), tls13_cr_build / tls13_ee_build / tls13_ext_key_share_hrr (hardcoded len==body), tls13_record_header / tls13_hs_header (big-endian length round-trip). ASN.1 DER ENCODE / OID base-128 encode: NO in-tree encoder exists — asn1_der.hexa is DECODE-ONLY ("Encoding-only side ignored") and x509_*.hexa are PARSERS (return slices), so the long-form 128-boundary / multi-byte-length / base-128-OID encoders the task scoped DO NOT EXIST to audit (nothing to break). BYTE-EQ: 3 source edits (ed25519+5, p256+6, record_io net+11; all closure-OUT — ed25519/p256/tls13_client_record_io NOT in build_selfhost) + 2 NEW leaves; net +18, no wipe_guard. LANE-2 (crypto ENCODE + verify-OOB guards) — no LANE-1 numeric overlap. $0, 0-pod, NO GPU, no vast, no foreign pod, no .tape, leak-0. F-OP109-CRYPTO-ENCODE-VERIFY-OOB) -->

[4] tls13_finished_verify (TLS1.3 handshake AUTH = HMAC-SHA256 over Expand-Label finished-key): len!=32 guard + accumulate-diff over 32B; tls13_client_verify_server_finished delegates to it. [5] tls13_record_open_verify: n<16 guard + delegates to [1]. [6] rsa_pkcs1_sha256_verify (RSA-2048 X.509 sig): STRICT RIGHT-ANCHORED EMSA parse (sep=elen-(DI+32)-1 FIXED, em[2..sep] all 0xFF, hash anchored at END) → DEFEATS BERserk/Bleichenbacher'06 garbage-tail forgery; Python port of EXACT parse + shipping lock: well-formed accept, garbage-tail/flipped-hash/padding-hole/wrong-blocktype ALL reject. [7] ed25519_verify: textbook k=H(R‖A‖M), pack(S·B+k·A)==R with A negated, _unpackneg rejects off-curve pubkey. [8] p256_verify: r,s∈[1,n-1] checks + R==O reject + (R.x mod n)==r. [9] x509_verify_self_ed25519: len(pk)==32 + len(sig)==64 + ed25519 over cert's OWN TBS. [10] x509_chain_verify_ed25519: per-link validity-window + sig under ISSUER pubkey (forged intermediate has no valid sig under real issuer key). [11] x509_validity_contains: notBefore<=now<=notAfter (expired → reject). [12] hmac_sha256_bytes: FIPS 198-1/180-4, byte-eq to runtime builtin. 🟠 NOTES: ed25519_verify/p256_verify have NO direct-call sig/pubkey length guard — all in-tree callers (x509_*) DO guard, so it's a defense-in-depth OOB robustness gap (LANE-1/OP-104-105 class), NOT a forgery-acceptance bug; flagged not fixed (scope). BYTE-EQ: ZERO source edits (every verifier already correct) + 1 NEW leaf; 0 deletions; crypto NOT in build_selfhost closure → fixpoint UNAFFECTED. LANE-2 only (no LANE-1 numeric overlap). 🟢 12-verifier verify/auth census: all reject forgery + constant-time; RSA right-anchored parse defeats BERserk; + 2 🟠 caller-guarded length notes + 1 non-security stale-compiler note. $0, 0-pod, NO GPU, no vast, no foreign pod, no .tape, leak-0. <!-- ANCHOR:OP-105-BINARY-OOB-SWEEP (unique anchor — LANE-2 0-pod systemic OOB-read census of the WHOLE length-prefixed/framed-binary parser family, generalizing OP-104's single cert_authorities fix. SURVEY (scoped greps, NO .git): TLS13 handshake/extension parsers stdlib/crypto/tls13_*.hexa, ASN.1 DER stdlib/crypto/asn1_der.hexa (TLV length→value), X.509 stdlib/crypto/x509_*.hexa, http2 stdlib/http2.hexa, websocket stdlib/net/websocket_native.hexa. For EACH inner copy/scan loop reading buf[base+j] for j<declared_len: is declared_len bounded by len(buf) before the read? CENSUS MATRIX (15 parser sites): 🔴 UNGUARDED→FIXED (9): tls13_ch_random (32B body[o+2+i]), tls13_ch_session_id (sl body[o+35+i]), tls13_ch_cipher_suites (cs_bytes body[cso+i]) in tls13_client_hello_parse.hexa; tls13_sh_random + tls13_sh_session_id in tls13_server_hello.hexa; tls13_cr_context (n body[1+i]) in tls13_cert_request.hexa; tls13_cert_entry_data (u24 n body[o+3+i]) in tls13_certificate.hexa; tls13_nst_nonce + tls13_nst_ticket (nl/tl body[...]) in tls13_new_session_ticket.hexa; der_value_len long-form (nbytes b[o+2+i]) + der_int (vlen b[voff+i]) + der_oid_str (vlen + INNER base-128 continuation loop with NO bound — a dangling high-bit byte reads past end) in asn1_der.hexa; x509_rsa_modulus (count spki[start+i]) + x509_rsa_exponent (e_len spki[e_v+i]) in x509_rsa.hexa; x509_p256 coord-copy (count b[start+i]) in x509_p256.hexa; x509_validity _dec (n b[off+i] ASCII time) in x509_validity.hexa. 🟢 GUARDED/CLEAN (note): tls13_alpn_parse (OP-104 reference, inner `j<nb && i+j<len`); tls13_key_share_parse_x25519 (`if len(ext_value) < 4+n { return [] }` BEFORE loop — the reference-quality guard); ws_recv_native (_ws_fill grows buffer to plen + `if len(pext)<plen {[]}` + `if plen>0 && len(body_bytes)<plen {return ""}` before unmask read); ws_frame_roundtrip_for_test (reads a LOCALLY-built frame, trusted self-consistent length — not untrusted wire); x509_p256_pubkey/sig fixed-64B reads (guarded by `if len(pk)!=64 {return false}`); x509_validity_contains d[6+i] (internally-built fixed 12-elem); the 2-byte-pair extension parsers OP-104 already certified. OUT-OF-SCOPE: stdlib/http2.hexa is a curl TEXT-output parser (shells to curl, parses strings) — NO binary frame/HPACK payload-copy loop exists (grep frame|24bit|<<16|hpack → 0). VERBATIM PRE-FIX REPROS on shipping `hexa run`: tls13_ch_session_id buggy + body[34]=32, len 35 → `index 35 out of bounds (len 35)`; der_value_len buggy + [0x30,0x82,0x01] (declares 2 len-bytes, 1 present) → `index 3 out of bounds (len 3)`; der_oid_str buggy + [0x2a,0x86] vlen=3 (dangling continuation) → `index 2 out of bounds (len 2)`; cr_context buggy + [4,0xAA,0xBB] (declared 4, 2 present) → `index 3 out of bounds (len 3)`. FIX (minimal, net-additive): each loop gains `&& base + i < len(buf)` mirroring OP-104/alpn — a truncated/malicious length yields a graceful SHORT/empty value, never an OOB fault. <!-- ANCHOR:OP-88-PATH-EXT-LEADING-DOT (unique anchor — LANE-2 bespoke-stdlib bug-hunt; OP-87 sibling. OP-87 fixed path_dirname's stray-trailing-slash on consecutive separators; this round hunts the SAME boundary-arithmetic bug class across the remaining path helpers + one more bespoke module. 🔴 REAL SHIPPING BUG FOUND+FIXED in stdlib/alloc/path.hexa path_ext (+ path_stem transitively): the last-'.' scan used a bare `while i > 0` guard that only protected a SINGLE leading dot, so it leaked a bogus extension whenever the basename began with a RUN of dots. POSIX/Python splitext treats the whole leading dot-run as name, never an extension. VERBATIM (faithful Python port of the UNFIXED body vs posixpath.splitext on basename): ext('..ext')='.ext' (want ''), ext('..')='.' (want ''), ext('...')='.' (want ''), ext('....y')='.y' (want ''), ext('a/..')='.' (want ''), ext('/..')='.' (want ''), ext('a/b/..')='.' (want ''). ROOT CAUSE: never identified the leading-dot-run boundary, so a '.' inside that prefix was mistaken for the ext separator. FIX (minimal, provably-correct — matches posixpath.splitext): skip the leading run of dots, then only treat a '.' AFTER it as the separator (`while lead<n {…}` + change `i>0`→`i>lead`; 0 deletions). Differential vs posixpath.splitext(basename) over 58 inputs: UNFIXED diverged on 10 leading-dot cases, FIXED matches 58/58 + NO ordinary regression (foo.txt→.txt, a/b.tar.gz→.gz, archive.TAR→.TAR, .bashrc→'', .config→'' unchanged). 🟢 CLEAN (other path helpers, differential): path_normalize 46-input sweep vs posixpath.normpath 0 real mism (the lone //a→/a is the POSIX-impl-defined leading-// case the docstring collapses; NOTE a naive '/'-join Python port FALSELY reported a doubled leading slash — hexa uses join('') so it's correct); path_join 15 pairs vs posixpath.join, only divergence join(a,'')→'a' is the DOCUMENTED empty-component contract (line 99), not a bug; path_basename re-confirmed GNU-correct. 🟢 CLEAN (invariant-lock) stdlib/semver.hexa (the one-more bespoke module, heaviest uncovered logic): semver_valid 40 inputs vs the OFFICIAL semver.org 2.0.0 regex 0 mism (leading-zero core/pre, empty pre segment, '_' reject, v-prefix, huge nums, +build); semver_compare 441 ordered pairs vs §11 precedence 0 mism (canonical alpha<alpha.1<alpha.beta<beta<beta.2<beta.11<rc.1<1.0.0 chain, numeric<alphanumeric, longer-list-wins); semver_satisfies 38 npm-style goldens 0 mism (^ ~ x-range || AND + prerelease-in-range gate). LANE split honored (stdlib/* only). 🔴→FIXED + 🟢. $0, 0-pod, NO GPU, no vast, no foreign pod, no .tape, leak-0. F-OP88-PATH-EXT-LEADING-DOT) -->

THE BUG (🔴): hex.hexa (lines 43,98) and utf8.hexa (line 142) both call bytes_to_str(out) to stringify a byte array, but bytes_to_str is defined ONLY in stdlib/ckpt/format.hexa (a `pub fn`, NOT a registered codegen builtin) and is NEVER imported by hex/utf8 — the EXACT OP-75 unregistered-builtin class. Every SIBLING multibyte codec EXPLICITLY noted this and inlined its own converter to dodge it (verbatim euc_kr.hexa:12-13 / cp949 / gbk / big5 / shift_jis / unicode_normalize: "utf8 references bytes_to_str (defined in stdlib/ckpt/format) without importing it, which only resolves in a full-stdlib build, not a standalone codec build"); hex and utf8 did NOT. FIX (source): 3 call sites bytes_to_str→bytes_to_str_raw (hex.hexa:43,98 + utf8.hexa:142); NEW stdlib/codec/hex_test.hexa (@sentinel __HEXA_CODEC_HEX__, RFC 4648 §8 vectors + full 0..255 byte sweep + case-insensitive + whitespace/colon + odd-length→[]) and stdlib/codec/utf8_test.hexa (@sentinel __HEXA_CODEC_UTF8__, RFC 3629 1/2/3/4-byte boundary vectors + round-trip + validity-detection + surrogate-skip), matching the stdlib/codec/* convention.   positive ordering AND the OP-52 swizzle-negative. (1) GAP#1 64x64 small-tile: at D<=512 the fixed
  128x128 tile covers only ~12% of the 132 SMs (16 tiles); a 64x64 tile gives 4x the tiles → 7 CTA/SM
  and lights up 4x more SMs. The cost model (a new device-COVERAGE wave_eff term for the under-fill
  regime) predicts MODE_t64 3.1x faster @D=256/512, with an HONEST crossover back to 128x128 by D~1024
  (where the 128x128 already covers ~half the device, so the small tile's lower per-tile issue_eff loses)
  and 128x128 still winning the filled parity regime D>=2048 — so the tile is ADDED without regressing
  any measured anchor. The 64x64 K-accumulation reuses MODE8's `for kk: WG(d0,dA,dB)` inner loop verbatim
  per sub-tile (NO split-K, NO reorder) → a future build is rel_rms 0 by construction. (2) GAP#4 host-side

  measured closed-neg) with a swz_penalty that applies OP-52's measured −1.6% to any swizzled mode in the
  compute-bound regime (tiles>=132). VAL-1: predicts the swizzle REGRESS at −1.6% (== the measured −1.6%)
  and the selector picks MODE8 not a swizzle @D=4096 — the OP-52 negative is ENCODED, not ignored. (3) the
  @D=4096 128x256-2CTA bigger-tile is SPECced + scored (~+8.5% partial close IF 2-CTA/SM is reg-achievable,
  NOT full parity vs cuBLAS ~427) and flagged as a FUTURE GPU-build with the bit-exact K-order constraint —
  OP-53 specs it, does not build it. Deliverables: docs/forge-routea-shape-adaptive.md §8-§9 ·
  self/native/wgmma/routea_cost_model.py (MODE_t64 + swz_penalty + select_config + 3 validations) ·
  F-OP53-SMALLTILE-LAUNCHER-DESIGN.txt. 0-pod, no GPU, no vast, no foreign pod, $0, leak-0.

  🟢 0-pod DESIGN (no GPU, no nvcc; all measured numbers CITED from F-OP45 + W-ladder verdicts). INVENTORY: 5

  D-dependent number). The gap 1.08x→1.50x decomposes EXACTLY into cuBLAS scaling UP +24.6% (342.5→426.8, shape-
  adaptive large-tile/split-K/persistent) + own scaling DOWN −9.9% (315.0→283.9, fixed 128x128 plain-launch, 2x

<!-- ANCHOR:OP-42-HEXFLOAT-CONTRACT-GATE (unique anchor — OP-40 (#3084) FIXED the comptime float const-fold to serialize folded doubles as bit-exact C99 hex-float literals (0x1.<mant>p<exp>, integer ops) instead of the lossy host %.17e path, closing a max-1-ULP residual to 0 across 125 cases. That is a determinism-relevant COMPILE-STEP guarantee whose discoverability + regression-lock were incomplete: OP-40's verdict explicitly deferred the contract clause ("docs milestone") and the OP-39 gate locked the fold bits but had no case NAMED for the hex-float path. OP-42 reflects the guarantee into the determinism contract (new compile-time-const-folding subsection) + extends the OP-39 gate with hex-float-specific cases — docs+test+workflow-comment only, NO codegen, NO .tape, g84 no-paper) -->
- [x] **OP-42 — OP-40 hex-float fold-serialize reflected into the determinism contract (new compile-time-const-folding §) + OP-39 const-fold gate extended with 5 hex-float regression cases (13→18)** —
  🟢 docs+gate, fully verified BOTH ways on the freshest local hexat (built from current source). CONTRACT
  (docs/flame-determinism-contract.md): new §1 subsection "compile-time constant folding — bit-exact hex-float serialize"
  framed as a COMPILE-STEP sibling of the 3 run-step layers — (a) compiler const-folds let-bound float-literal exprs at
  comptime + inlines the folded literal at every use-site; (b) RULE: folded const serialized as a bit-exact C99 hex-float
  literal (0x1.<mant>p<exp>, integer ops only) so clang re-parses ZERO-loss, NOT decimal %g/%e (hand-rolled formatter not
  correctly-rounded → drifts); (c) WHY: a 1-ULP fold drift makes the SAME source compile to different float bytes per host's

  — products the OLD %.17e/%g serialize mis-rounded (MUL_HF1 0.254829592*0.3275911 emitted lossy 0.0834799 pre-fix vs exact

  current source (tool/regen_cc_manual, HEXA_V2=Jun-8 hexat + restored frozen runtime.c) → emits hex-floats, self-tests

  verbatim into a standalone C harness vs host libc snprintf + python correctly-rounded ground truth): hxlcl_vsnprintf
  %.17g does NOT round-trip to the same double in 3463/5536 (62.6%), differs from libc in 5057/5536 (91.3%); %.17e
  parses to a DIFFERENT double than libc in 3432/5536 (62.0%), MAX 5 ULP (OP-40's narrow A&S sweep saw only 1 ULP —
  the wide range is worse). libc round-trips 100% (0/5536, sanity). → shortest-round-trip-INCORRECT, REAL + PERVASIVE
  (every high-precision float print: println/loss/grad-norm logs/to_string). ROOT = naive digit-extraction
  (`d=(int)dv; dv=(dv-d)*10.0` accumulates fp error; round-half-up peek-1-digit; no Grisu/Ryū; capped 18 digits),
  frozen self/runtime.c:722. CORRECT FIX = route the float branch to libc snprintf (OP-37b's atof→strtod pattern in
  REVERSE; libc %e/%g are C-standard correctly-rounded AND machine-independent → cross-platform deterministic). PROVEN:
  same 5521-case sweep with the float branch delegating to libc snprintf → 0/5521 round-trip fail, 0/5521 drift. libc
  IS reachable (runtime #includes <stdio.h>/<math.h> + already calls 36 real libm cos/sin/exp directly per
  stdlib_trig_libm; the hxlcl_* layer is deliberate symbol-elimination, not freestanding-hard). DEFERRED 0-pod: the
  formatter body lives ONLY in the gitignored frozen-seed self/runtime.c (restored from IMMUTABLE anchor 151c52c82;
  NO tracked SSOT emitter — codegen.hexa refs are comments; runtime.c.hexanoport: "Cannot be ported to .hexa (it IS

  — worst gap is at step 3-5 (EARLY transient), late-half (steps>250) worst is SMALLER (3.2e-6 / 2.2e-7)
  and flat, step-500 tracking 1.1e-7 (B=8) to 3.4e-6 (B=1), NO late blow-up; (2) the LR schedule does NOT

  chaotic-but-bounded (5x more steps + harder target + noisy B=1), EXACTLY why LOSS not weights is the
  decisive metric (butterfly drifts weights; loss tracks). VERDICT: TF32 fast-mode HOLDS at the longer/
  harsher horizon — training-equivalent (bounded loss-tracking) to >=N=500 under an LR schedule; strengthens

- [x] **OP-25 — deterministic BF16 fast-mode: self-byte-eq + W14-tol + speed vs TF32 (precision Pareto, aiden)** —
  GREEN gates / DOMINATED outcome (honest closed result). Probed the precision-uncap ladder's NEXT rung after

  FREE pool, 8/8 1-step cells + 4/4 drift cells (DEFAULT+PEDANTIC × D={768,1536} × B={1,8}). (1) BF16 SELF-BYTE-EQ
  YES — max|delta(W',loss)|=EXACTLY 0 every cell + over the whole 50-step trajectory; PEDANTIC NOT needed (DEFAULT
  bf16 tensor-op already deterministic, identical bytes+time — same as TF32/OP-20). (2) rel-RMS vs FP64 ~1.1e-6
  (NOT the expected ~1e-3) — because fp32 MASTER weights mean only GEMM operands are bf16, so the bf16 e-3 GEMM
  error enters W via one tiny optimizer step → e-6; essentially EQUAL to TF32's 1.13e-6, well inside W14 1e-2.
  (3) SPEED: BF16/FP64 = 3.88-4.10× @B=1 (same as TF32); BF16-vs-TF32 = TF32/BF16 1.01-1.12× → BF16 at most ~12%
  faster @B=8 (half-input-bytes mem traffic, NOT compute) and a DEAD HEAT @B=1 (1.01-1.02×, the latency regime the
  ~3× cap names). On the 5070 BF16 & TF32 are both 16-bit-input tensor-ops at EQUAL throughput → no GEMM-cost edge.
  (4) DRIFT N=50: BF16 LOSS TRACKS FP64 (worst loss-track gap 9.4e-5/1.7e-4, bounded, no peel; weight rel-RMS ~e-6,
  does NOT grow — chaotic-but-microscopic, same shape as OP-23's TF32 drift) → real trainable fast-mode, not a
  1-step illusion. PARETO: FP64(exact,1×) → TF32(e-6,4.2×) → BF16(e-6,4.1× = SAME accuracy + SAME speed as TF32).
  ==> BF16 is Pareto-DOMINATED by TF32: not worse, but not better on either axis → no reason to prefer it. TF32
  stays the precision-uncap TERMINAL SWEET SPOT; the BF16 rung is a NO-OP on consumer hardware. Verdict

- [x] **OP-23 — TF32 N-step trajectory drift vs FP64: validate TF32 fast-mode is real not 1-step illusion (aiden)** —
  GREEN. Decisively VALIDATED: OP-20's deterministic TF32 fast-mode is a REAL training fast-mode, NOT a 1-step
  illusion. Ran TWO continuous trajectories (TF32 + FP64) from the SAME seed/data for N=100 steps (AdamW state

  D={768,1536}, B={1,8}). (1) LOSS-TRACKING = YES, decisive: TF32 loss tracks FP64 loss to ~1e-7 every step;
  WORST gap 2.5e-5 is at the COLD-START step 1, then DROPS to ~1e-7 and stays flat — NO peeling, NO drift trend,
  both lanes converge to the SAME loss along the SAME curve. (2) WEIGHT rel-RMS = BOUNDED ~5e-7 (starts 1.13e-6
  = OP-20 1-step #, then SHRINKS to ~4.5-5.3e-7 by step 100; does NOT grow) — chaotic-but-microscopic (3-4 orders
  inside NN's ~1e-3 forgiveness). (3) TF32 self-byte-eq over the WHOLE trajectory: run1-vs-run2 W max|Δ|=0 AND
  per-step loss max|Δ|=0 at step N (determinism holds across the trajectory, not just step 1; PEDANTIC not needed).
  HONEST (g5): the RIGHT metric is loss-tracking (training-equiv), NOT weight byte-closeness — chaos guarantees
  weights drift, that's why flame's identity is SELF-determinism (TF32-vs-TF32=0), not cross-precision. So:
  bounded loss-tracking = real fast-mode CONFIRMED. Caveat: N=100 small synthetic config (mean(G²) loss proxy,

  NVCC_MISSING clean early-exit (instructions); NO_GPU clean early-exit when nvidia-smi absent/empty OR compute_cap

  NVCC_MISSING and NO_GPU paths exit clean (code 1) with copy-paste instructions and the DESTROY trap fires. (b) the
  OP-29 (#3042) FMA cross-cut: AUDITED w16's gate references — WROTE tool/wgmma/w16_op29_ref_check.cpp (clang++,
  0-pod) proving MODE-1's CPU reference is the OP-29-SAFE inline ascending `acc += a*b` (NOT fmaf-fused; mul+add are
  separate statements so -ffp-contract cannot synthesize an FMA; deterministic re-run; == w16.cu L558 verbatim; the

  CUBLAS_TF32_TENSOR_OP_MATH, w16.cu L645-647) with NO CPU FMA at all — so the OP-29 cross-ISA FMA-divergence failure
  mode is STRUCTURALLY ABSENT from the MODE-4 gate and CANNOT false-fail it; the device wgmma K-accumulation order is
  well-defined (ascending k, slab-order == flat-order 16384/16384). RAN locally: 4/4 PASS, exit 0. HONEST (g5): the

  RESULT (all 8 PASS): (a) GATE-A TF32 self-byte-eq run-to-run max|delta(W')| = EXACTLY 0 — pedantic-cublas
  NOT needed (default tensor-op TF32 is already deterministic on the 5070; PEDANTIC gives identical bytes at
  identical time → recommend PEDANTIC as the portable SHIP guarantee). (b) GATE-B rel-RMS(TF32 vs FP64 W') ~
  1.13e-6 — 4 orders inside W14 1e-2. (c) SPEED FP64/TF32 = 4.19-4.63x @B=1, 19-21x @B=8 → BREAKS the ~3x cap
  at every shape (B=1 latency-bound — the regime the cap was named for — is already 4.2-4.6x). HONEST: the
  B=8 ~20x is INFLATED by the 5070's crippled FP64 (~1/64 FP32); a datacenter card would show less — quote
  B=1 (4.2x, card-robust) as the headline; determinism proven for THIS card/cuBLAS-13.0 (pin PEDANTIC to
  guarantee portably). Deterministic TF32 fast-mode = a REAL flame fast-mode: identity kept + W14-equivalent
  + >3x faster. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP20-TF32-FASTMODE.txt. $0, FREE aiden, no vast/pod/leak.

  runtime.a) of CE-bwd clm_ce_grad's grad in BOTH libm-exp + dt_exp-Taylor form. RAN ON 3 PLATFORMS: local +
  ghost (arm64-macos) vs aiden (x86-linux) — cross-arch AND cross-OS. VERDICT: libm-exp CEBWD fold DIVERGED
  (arm64-macos 7969105254299072804 ≠ x86-linux 3352931952497630952) while dt_exp was byte-IDENTICAL on all 3.
  ISOLATED via per-element byte diff: EXACTLY 4 of 4096 grad elems differ, EACH by 1 mantissa-LSB = 1 ULP

  dt_exp (matching CE-fwd nn_ce_loss_allpos) on host (clm_prod.hexa) AND the GPU kernel (_hx_dt_exp_dev in
  runtime_cuda_emit.hexa, the _moe_exp_dev precedent → host↔device byte-eq holds + device also deterministic).
  Grad-change magnitude: max abs 2.17e-18, max rel ≈2.0e-14 (a few ULPs) — trades "matches libm" for "matches
  across ALL platforms" (g5 honest). AFTER: production CE-bwd fold = 7679248634312321699 IDENTICAL on all 3 →
  cross-platform byte-identical YES. OP-11 oracle RE-LOCKED (clm_prod_ce_softmax_grad_eq.hexa _ce_grad_prod +
  _ce_grad_ref libm→dt_exp): F-OP11 = 1 PASS, all max|Δ|=0. Contract doc updated (3 exp impls → 2). RESIDUAL
  (honest latent, not closed): GELU libm `erf` (fwd+bwd) is the same kind of hole but no bit-accurate
  deterministic erf exists in-tree (A&S 7.1.26 is 1.5e-7-off + itself libm-exp-dependent) AND `erf` won't link
  on aiden's runtime — documented as follow-up. $0, 0-GPU, free pool, no vast. Verdict
  .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP19-CROSSPLATFORM-EXACT.txt.

<!-- ANCHOR:OP-19B-DET-ERF (unique anchor — seals the GELU erf cross-platform hole OP-19 deferred; the LAST libm transcendental in the step) -->
- [x] **OP-19b — pure-FP deterministic erf seals GELU → flame FULLY machine-independent byte-exact (0-GPU)** —
  Closed OP-19's measured latent residual: the GELU `erf` path (fwd `GELU(x)=x·0.5·(1+erf(x/√2))` + bwd

  exp routed through OP-19's deterministic `dt_exp` Taylor — pure +,-,*,/ + dt_exp, **NO libm, NO other
  transcendental**. KEY: BRANCHLESS in z (only the z=0 odd sign flip). A first cut used a Maclaurin series +
  hard clamp at |z|≥4; that PIECEWISE form broke max|Δ|=0 because the GELU argument straddles the branch
  boundary under in-register-vs-stored-reload rounding (measured 2e-7 fused≠unfused). The unconditional A&S form
  has no value-dependent boundary to straddle → byte-eq restored. max|dt_erf − libm erf| = 1.38e-7 (≤ GELU
  tolerance; honest g5 — trades "matches libm erf" for "matches across ALL platforms"). Wired host (nn_lib
  `_nn_normal_cdf`/`_pdf` + gn_lib `_gn_gelu`) + reference (clm_conv_devfeed) + device (`_hx_dt_erf_dev` shared by
  `_hx_k_gelu`/gelu2/`_hx_gelu_dev`/gelu_bwd; `_hx_dt_exp_dev` hoisted) + host C fallback (restore_frozen_seeds
  `_op18_gelu` → dt_erf, BYTE-IDENTICAL to the hexa dt_erf, fold 93,35,192,253,183,12,237,63 @1.19071). CROSS-
  PLATFORM ORACLE (stdlib/flame/op19b_crossplatform_erf.hexa, self-contained): det-erf GELU fwd+bwd byte fold
  IDENTICAL on local + ghost (arm64-macos) AND aiden (x86-linux) — FWD 4548590605583584556, BWD
  4249661408190172843 on all 3. BEFORE (honest): libm `erf` won't even LINK on aiden (`hexa_math_erf` undefined)
  so a libm-erf GELU oracle CANNOT be cross-platform measured — the same hole-class OP-19 measured for libm exp
  (1 ULP arm64↔x86). DEPENDENT ORACLES re-locked: OP-9 LN-reduction (self-contained _ln_gelu→dt_erf) PASS 0.0;
  GN-GELU fusion re-lock proof (op19b_gngelu_relock.hexa, both arms dt_erf) PASS max|Δ|=0; OP-15 step + OP-18
  gelu2 inherit via nn_lib + the dt_erf host fallback (re-lock on fresh build — local hexa is a stale prebuilt
  that uses its own embedded stdlib, so the production-stdlib oracles validate on CI rebuild). RESULT: with
  OP-19's dt_exp + OP-19b's dt_erf, the flame CLMConvMoE step has **NO libm transcendental left** (exp/erf/ln all
  hand-rolled deterministic; sqrt already Newton) → flame is now FULLY machine-independent byte-exact. $0, 0-GPU,
  free pool (aiden/ghost), no vast. Verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP19B-DET-ERF.txt.

  constructor" ABI; **musl passes NO args to ctors** → envp garbage → `hxlcl_environ`=garbage → segfault before main.
  Worked around with a DISCLOSED TEST-ONLY runtime copy (ctor reads musl's `extern __environ`; env-capture ONLY, all
  math byte-identical; NOT committed). RESULT — **4-ENVIRONMENT byte-IDENTICAL**: musl dt_exp = 7679248634312321699,
  dt_erf FWD = 4548590605583584556, BWD = 4249661408190172843 — all match the recorded Darwin+glibc-x86+glibc-arm64
  values bit-for-bit. # DISTINCT libm impls spanned = **3 (glibc·musl·Darwin)**: libm `erf` gives 4 DIFFERENT values
  across the 4 envs (musl 7314648833623304241 ≠ glibc-x86 6306829276275644424 ≠ glibc-arm64 3332333775004383127 ≠
  Darwin 1521224270287218303) while dt_erf is identical on all → DEFINITIVE that ONLY the deterministic dt_* path is
  machine-independent. NO divergence/defect on the production path (the musl init segfault is a libc-ABI env-capture
  bug hit BEFORE any fold math — flagged as a runtime follow-up). $0, 0-GPU, free pool (summer docker + Alpine), ZERO

<!-- ANCHOR:OP-19E-MUSL-ENVFIX (unique anchor — the durable runtime fix for the OP-19d musl ctor-ABI SIGSEGV: POSIX `environ` global replaces the glibc/Darwin-only (argc,argv,envp) constructor-args ABI; upgrades OP-19d's test-only shim to a real native-musl run) -->
- [x] **OP-19e — musl-safe env-capture (POSIX environ, not constructor-args ABI); fixes the OP-19d SIGSEGV (0-pod)** —
  THE durable fix for the REAL hexa-runtime↔musl bug OP-19d surfaced. The priority-101 `hxlcl_capture_environ(int argc,
  char**argv, char**envp)` ctor relied on the glibc/Darwin-only "(argc,argv,envp)→constructor" ABI; **musl passes NO
  args to ctors** → `envp` = garbage register → `hxlcl_environ`=garbage → SIGSEGV in `_hexa_init_mem_cap`→`hxlcl_getenv`
  BEFORE main(). FIX: read the POSIX global `extern char **environ` (defined by EVERY libc incl. musl) instead — ctor
  still runs at prio 101 (`hxlcl_capture_environ(void){ hxlcl_environ = environ; }`), `environ` referenced before the
  `#define environ hxlcl_environ` shadow so it binds the libc symbol. BEHAVIOR-PRESERVING on glibc/Darwin (ctor-arg envp
  and libc `environ` point at the SAME vector at start → same env captured), FIXES musl (real pointer). DURABLE LANDING:
  self/runtime.c is gitignored (frozen seed 151c52c8…), so the fix lands via the OP-16/17/18 mechanism — an idempotent,

  every restore (the ONE tracked file; runtime.c stays untracked). PROOF (0-pod, summer docker + Alpine, $0, NO vast/GPU):
  (a) isolated reproducer native — Alpine/musl OLD ctor-ABI = SIGSEGV exit 139, NEW POSIX-environ = clean exit 0; glibc
  OLD≡NEW identical; Darwin clean. (b) full patched self/runtime.c builds under musl (runtime.o OK, ZERO environ diags)
  + Darwin (`-fsyntax-only` exit 0). (c) BONUS — real native-musl `hexa run` of op19_crossplatform_selfcontained.hexa
  against the patched runtime, NO SHIM: `ldd → libc.musl-x86_64.so.1`, **RUN_EXIT=0** (SIGSEGV GONE), deterministic
  Taylor folds **byte-identical across Darwin + glibc(summer) + native-musl** (dt_exp 7679248634312321699, dt_erf FWD
  4548590605583584556, GELUBWD 636106759170901885 — all three equal on all three), while every libm-* line DIVERGES

  .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP19E-MUSL-ENVFIX.txt.

  `<secure/_string.h>`/`_strings.h`/`_stdio.h` ALREADY `#define` strcat/bzero/memcpy/memset/memmove/strncpy/
  strcpy/snprintf/sprintf as `__*_chk_func` fortify macros (pulled in transitively by runtime.c's top
  `#include <string.h>`/`<strings.h>`/`<stdio.h>`); (2) self/runtime.c's "Textual override" libc-interception
  block (frozen-seed lines 2070,2082-2087,2095-2096) redefines those same 9 names to the `hxlcl_*` svc-trap
  helpers → 9 [-Wmacro-redefined]. (Only these 9 collide — the other override names strlen/memcmp/strcmp/… are
  plain externs, not macros.) MINIMAL FIX: `#undef <NAME>` the 9 names right before the override block — the
  EXACT precedent the seed already uses for `#undef isalnum`/`#undef exit` two screens down. BEHAVIOR-PRESERVING
  (PROVEN via `clang -E`): our hxlcl_* `#define` is the LAST definition either way, so the effective expansion is
  byte-identical before vs after — `#undef` only silences the warning (and is a standards no-op on Linux where
  glibc doesn't macro-define these → platform-neutral). LOCAL VERIFY (0-GPU, `clang -fsyntax-only -DHEXA_RT_SELFEMIT`):
  -Wmacro-redefined 9→0, the 2 unrelated pre-existing warning classes (4 -Wincompatible-pointer + 12
  -Wundefined-internal) UNCHANGED, 0 errors. HONEST landing (g5, OP-2b/OP-15/OP-16 class): self/runtime.c is
  gitignored frozen-seed (#2065 .c-graduation, restored from immutable blob 151c52c8… — no tracked emit SSOT), so
  the durable fix lands as a deterministic, idempotent, marker-guarded POST-RESTORE PATCH in the TRACKED
  tool/restore_frozen_seeds (injects the 9 `#undef`s on every restore) → every build env (CI/release/local
  bootstrap) gets the de-duplicated runtime.c automatically. End-to-end verified through the patched tool. 9
  warnings GONE · behavior-preserving YES · no new warn YES · GPU/pod/vast NONE ($0). Verdict
  .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP17-MACRO-REDEF.txt.

  1.005x (567->570 GB/s) and an fp32 AdamW with the literal .v4 float4 lever is only 1.028x — on the 5070
  (sm_120, GDDR7) the memory controller ALREADY coalesces contiguous scalar 32/64-bit grid-stride accesses

  partially-uncoalesced smem-feed loads to repair; a contiguous elementwise/copy kernel has none → the

  gate. NOT shipped (no win + not byte-eq under default flags). Contiguous-elementwise vectorization lever

- [x] **OP-6b — fuse the AdamW update INTO the bwd-GEMM epilogue (boundary-removal, not vectorization)** —
  OP-6's deferred follow-up: fold _hx_k_adamw_step into the bwd dW GEMM's epilogue so dW never round-trips
  through DRAM as a separate 7-stream kernel + launch. BWD-dW PATH DETERMINED = SCOPE B (cuBLAS-bound):
  conv1d_bwd_via_forge (clm_prod.hexa:238) computes dW via forge_dispatch_matmul → farr_matmul_gpu → REAL
  cuBLAS Dgemm (runtime_cuda_emit.hexa) — a CLOSED cuBLAS call you cannot fuse an epilogue into; boundary-
  removal is only EXPRESSIBLE on an own-GEMM bwd path. CLOSED-NEGATIVE (honest, aiden RTX 5070 sm_120, GPU 0%):
  built a scope-A demonstration (fp64 tiled own-GEMM, fused gemm_dW_adamw_fused consumes dW in-register before
  the C-store + applies verbatim ADAMW_BODY, vs separate gemm_dW_store + adamw_separate = dW DRAM round-trip +
  2nd launch). PERF: ~1.000-1.002x on production-realistic GEMM-dominated shapes (dW round-trip eliminated is
  Amdahl-negligible vs the GEMM), and SLIGHTLY SLOWER 0.98x in the dW-dominated regime (large M,N tiny K) —
  the fused epilogue runs the W,M,V elementwise work under the GEMM's TILE=16 geometry at WORSE bandwidth than
  a dedicated 256-thread AdamW kernel, outweighing the ~40-70 GB/s of dW traffic saved. Fusion wins ONLY in a
  tiny-GEMM launch-bound regime (1.108x @0.02ms step) where killing the 2nd LAUNCH matters. BIT-EXACT (g5,
  STRONGER than OP-6): fused W,M,V == separate W,M,V max|Δ|=0 bitdiff=0 under BOTH --fmad=false AND --fmad=true
  at every shape — register-source fusion does NOT reschedule the AdamW FMAs (the gradient source changes,
  not the arithmetic order), so the byte-eq concern that blocked OP-6's vectorization does NOT apply. NOT
  shipped (no win + scope B cuBLAS). Boundary-removal pays only when a side is UNDER-utilized; here neither the
  bwd GEMM nor the AdamW is under-filled → nothing to recover. Elementwise lever now EXHAUSTED on BOTH axes
  (OP-6 instruction-width, OP-6b boundary-removal). Harness tool/op6b/op6b_adamw_fuse_bench.cu · verdict
  .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP6B-ADAMW-FUSE.txt.

- [x] **OP-9 — byte-eq CPU oracle for the groupnorm/LN valley reduction (0-GPU)** — continuing the
  OP-2/OP-7/OP-8 determinism-oracle series, added a LOCAL `hexa run` (0-GPU) oracle that bit-exactly locks the
  flame CLMConvMoE GroupNorm "valley" normalization the FUSED hot path (HEXA_FUSE_VALLEY / HEXA_FUSE_GN_GELU)
  relies on. The production reduction (gn_lib nn_groupnorm_fwd / nn_gn_gelu_fused) is a TWO-PASS mean/variance
  (NOT Welford): pass-1 sum=Σ X → mu, pass-2 vs=Σ(X-mu)² → var, both over the SAME (t-OUTER,c-INNER) order
  (sequential, NO tree re-assoc); inv=1/_gn_sqrt(var+eps), eps=1e-5; Y=gamma·xhat+beta; A=GELU(Y) (erf CDF).
  OP-9 proves the UN-FUSED form (nn_groupnorm_fwd: two-pass reduction + SEPARATE affine sweep writing Y, THEN
  SEPARATE GELU sweep re-reading Y → A) == the FUSED VALLEY form (nn_gn_gelu_fused: SAME reduction, but affine
  +GELU in ONE pass — post-GN [T·C] touched ONCE, no Y read+write round-trip), max(|ΔY|,|ΔA|)=0. Both use the
  SAME two-pass (t-outer,c-inner) reduction order, SAME _gn_sqrt (40-iter Newton), SAME erf-GELU ⇒ a true
  fusion/boundary-removal identity, NOT an associativity case (no tolerance). HONEST (g5): the tree-vs-
  sequential associativity RISK is REAL but does NOT arise — the fusion only collapses the GN-affine+GELU
  elementwise sweeps, it does NOT re-associate the mean/var sum, so the CPU oracle matches the production
  sequential order EXACTLY → genuine max|Δ|=0, no eps. CANONICAL ORDER = sequential (t-outer,c-inner) two-pass
  mean-then-var (device kernel = SSOT); a future warp-shuffle/tree reduce or Welford switch would trip this
  oracle. `hexa run` PASS, max|Δ|=0 across 7 shapes (G=1 LN-degenerate, G=2/3/4/8, varied T,C, + T=1 pure
  cross-channel + cg=1 per-channel edges). Behavior-preserving: NO trainer logic changed. Oracle
  stdlib/flame/clm_prod_ln_reduction_eq.hexa · verdict .verdicts/hexa-0pod/F-OP9-LN-REDUCTION-ORACLE.txt.

- [x] **OP-10 — CPU oracle characterizing the B>1 causal-conv window-concat seam (0-GPU)** — made the
  flame_h100_h200_closeout's KNOWN honest non-bit-exact spot PRECISE. The flame batched step
  (CLM_PROD_BATCH=B) concatenates B distinct length-Tw windows into ONE length-T=B*Tw buffer and runs the
  causal-dilated Conv1d over the whole thing; the closeout flagged a "K-1 causal-conv SEAM-only Δ" vs a
  per-window-segmented conv. This LOCAL `hexa run` (0-GPU) oracle computes BOTH paths on CPU — (a) the
  flame concat conv (every previous-window row visible to the receptive field p=t-dil*(K-1-k)) vs (b) a
  per-window-segmented reference that zeros the cross-window causal context — and maps Δ per output
  position. FINDING (g5, honest CHARACTERIZATION not max|Δ|=0-everywhere): the INTERIOR is bit-exact
  (interior max|Δ|=0, 0 bad positions across 6 cases) and the SEAM is EXACTLY the first (K-1)*dil output
  positions of every window AFTER the first, where Δ = the cross-window context the segmented form zeros
  (genuinely nonzero, 0 mischaracterized). CONFIRMS the closeout claim and REFINES it: dil=1 ⇒ band=K-1
  (the named case); dil>1 ⇒ band=(K-1)*dil (the trunk's dilated convs widen the seam — the closeout said a

  decoupled-wd first); c1,c2=1−βᵗ with βᵗ by repeated-mul (not pow). `hexa run` PASS, max|Δ|=0 all 7 cases.

## honest framing (g5)

Free-resource-only loop: every gate runs on the sidecar pool (aiden/summer 5070) or locally — NO vast cost.
Bit-exact / byte-eq discipline holds (the consumer card preserves flame's identity). When a milestone genuinely
needs a Hopper H100 (sm_90a wgmma), it is DEFERRED here (out of 0-pod scope), not faked. The loop drains this
backlog and self-feeds new 0-pod milestones as they surface.
